# Geophysics

↑ **Parent:** [Branches of physics](physics.md#branches-of-physics)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geophysics)

Geophysics applies [physics](physics.md) and [mathematics](mathematics.md) to the structure, composition, and evolution of [planets](planetary-science.md#planet), including [Earth](planetary-science.md#earth). It connects measurements with mechanisms: [plate tectonics](#plate-tectonics) explains moving crust, the [geodynamo](planetary-science.md#geodynamo) explains a planetary [magnetic field](magnetic-field.md), and [albedo](#albedo) connects reflected sunlight to a [planetary surface energy balance](#planetary-surface-energy-balance). Even a small model can reveal a useful organizing principle, such as the competition between [ice-ocean albedo feedback](#ice-ocean-albedo-feedback) and stabilizing [thermal radiation](electromagnetic-radiation.md#thermal-radiation).

## Climatology

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climatology)

### Climatological normal

↑ **Parent:** [Climatology](#climatology)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climatological_normal)

A climate statistic calculated over a stated long reference period, conventionally often 30 years. Specify the variable, site, period and averaging procedure; a changing climate makes the choice of baseline consequential.[Conventional 30-year normals](physics-bibliography.md#u-s-climate-normals-by-noaa)

## Rock physics

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

The study of how rock composition, structure, pressure, [temperature](temperature.md), fluids and deformation determine measurable physical properties such as [density](fluid-mechanics.md#density), elastic wave speeds, electrical conductivity and attenuation. These relations are needed to interpret [seismic tomography](inverse-problem.md#seismic-tomography) and [gravity anomalies](#gravity-anomaly); one measured property rarely determines a unique material state.

## British Geological Survey

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Geological_Survey)

British geological survey organization publishing geological and [earthquake](#earthquake) observations and explanatory material. [https://www.bgs.ac.uk](https://www.bgs.ac.uk)

### Work by British Geological Survey

↑ **Parent:** [British Geological Survey](#british-geological-survey)

## Magnetotelluric sounding

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

An electromagnetic method using naturally varying fields to infer subsurface [electrical resistivity](electric-charge.md#electrical-resistivity); conductive anomalies require geological interpretation.

## Borehole breakout

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

Failure and widening of a borehole wall under concentrated [stress](continuum-mechanics.md#stress); its orientation helps constrain in situ principal [stress](continuum-mechanics.md#stress) directions.

## Thermal structure

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

The spatial distribution of [temperature](temperature.md) in a region, such as a [slab](#slab) and its surrounding [mantle wedge](#mantle-wedge).

## UBC Geophysical Inversion Facility

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

Institutional teaching source for applied geophysical survey methods.

### Work by UBC Geophysical Inversion Facility

↑ **Parent:** [UBC Geophysical Inversion Facility](#ubc-geophysical-inversion-facility)

## Geometrics

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

Instrument manufacturer and issuer of manuals for field [magnetometers](electromagnetism.md#magnetometer).

### Work by Geometrics

↑ **Parent:** [Geometrics](#geometrics)

## Magnetic survey

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

Measurement of a [magnetic field](magnetic-field.md) over a region to locate contrasts in subsurface [magnetization](electromagnetism.md#magnetization). Temporal background changes and cultural interference must be distinguished from spatial [magnetic anomalies](#magnetic-anomaly).

### Base-station magnetometer

↑ **Parent:** [Magnetic survey](#magnetic-survey)

A stationary [magnetometer](electromagnetism.md#magnetometer) recording time variation of the background [magnetic field](magnetic-field.md) while a moving instrument measures spatial variation. Subtracting the synchronized base changes reduces temporal contamination.

### Proton-precession magnetometer

↑ **Parent:** [Magnetic survey](#magnetic-survey)

An absolute scalar [magnetometer](electromagnetism.md#magnetometer) that infers $|\mathbf B|$ from the free [Larmor precession](angular-momentum-operator.md#larmor-precession) frequency of polarized [protons](physics.md#proton). It measures field strength rather than field direction.

## Paleomagnetism

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

Study of ancient magnetic-field records preserved in rocks. Primary magnetizations can constrain former latitude and rotation, subject to age and alteration tests.

### Remagnetization

↑ **Parent:** [Paleomagnetism](#paleomagnetism)

Acquisition of a younger [remanent magnetization](electromagnetism.md#remanent-magnetization) that partly or completely replaces an older magnetic record. Heating, chemical alteration or later mineral growth can cause it. A successful [continental reconstruction](planetary-science.md#continental-reconstruction) must connect the age of the measured magnetic component to the geological event being reconstructed.

### Paleomagnetic reversal test

↑ **Parent:** [Paleomagnetism](#paleomagnetism)

Test whether normal- and reversed-polarity [remanent magnetization](electromagnetism.md#remanent-magnetization) directions are statistically antipodal after suitable structural correction. Agreement supports recording of opposite polarities of the same large-scale [magnetic field](magnetic-field.md); failure can reveal overprints or other complications. Passing is not, by itself, proof of primary acquisition.

### Paleomagnetic fold test

↑ **Parent:** [Paleomagnetism](#paleomagnetism)

Compare the scatter of [remanent magnetization](electromagnetism.md#remanent-magnetization) directions before and after correcting for [folding](planetary-science.md#folding). Improved agreement after restoring bedding supports acquisition before [folding](planetary-science.md#folding); agreement only in present coordinates supports a younger overprint. Partial unfolding can indicate acquisition during deformation or more complicated histories. The test constrains relative timing, rather than supplying an absolute age.

### Apparent polar wander path

↑ **Parent:** [Paleomagnetism](#paleomagnetism)

A time-ordered sequence of ancient magnetic pole positions inferred from dated [remanent magnetization](electromagnetism.md#remanent-magnetization) in a continental block. The apparent motion includes the block's movement relative to the rotation axis and can include [true polar wander](planetary-science.md#true-polar-wander). Consistent paths after a fixed relative rotation support an interval of shared motion; conventional [paleomagnetism](#paleomagnetism) does not independently determine absolute longitude.

## Geodynamic modelling

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

Quantitative simulation of evolving planetary deformation, flow and thermal or compositional structure under specified physical laws and constitutive assumptions. Feasibility of a regime does not supply its historical onset date.

## Air parcel

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

An [air parcel](#air-parcel) is a small material volume followed through an atmospheric flow. The elementary parcel model assumes rapid pressure adjustment to its environment but negligible heat and material exchange during the displacement. Its [potential temperature](#potential-temperature) is conserved during reversible dry [adiabatic process](first-law-of-thermodynamics.md#adiabatic-process) at fixed composition.

## Regolith

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

Loose fragmented material above coherent bedrock. On the Moon its fractures and debris strongly scatter seismic waves, complicating detection of weak deep-interior [phases](wave.md#phase-waves).

## Geopotential

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geopotential)

The [gravitational potential energy](energy.md#gravitational-energy) per unit [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass), including the centrifugal contribution when using the effective gravity of a rotating planet. In a local atmosphere with approximately constant effective [gravitational acceleration](newton-s-law-of-universal-gravitation.md#gravitational-acceleration), $\Phi=gz$ up to a constant. Its horizontal [gradient](multivariable-calculus.md#gradient) on an [isobaric surface](geophysical-fluid-dynamics.md#isobaric-surface) drives the balanced wind.

### Geopotential surface

↑ **Parent:** [Geopotential](#geopotential)

A surface on which [geopotential](#geopotential) is constant. Effective [gravity](physics.md#gravity) is perpendicular to it. In a local constant-gravity approximation these surfaces are horizontal; comparing their slopes with [isopycnals](fluid-mechanics.md#isopycnal) identifies the geometry permitting an [adiabatic parcel exchange](hydrodynamic-stability.md#adiabatic-parcel-exchange) to release [available potential energy](energy.md#available-potential-energy).

## Climate

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate)

The statistical properties of atmospheric and related oceanic conditions over an extended period, including means, variability and extremes. [Radiation budgets](#radiation-budget), [ocean circulation](geophysical-fluid-dynamics.md#ocean-circulation) and atmospheric transport help determine its large-scale structure.

### Paleoclimate

↑ **Parent:** [Climate](#climate)

The climate of an earlier geological interval, inferred from dated geological, geochemical or biological indicators. Spatially coherent glacial deposits, striations and climate-sensitive rocks can test a [continental reconstruction](planetary-science.md#continental-reconstruction), provided their ages and original tectonic contexts are established.

### Rain shadow

↑ **Parent:** [Climate](#climate)

A region of reduced precipitation on the leeward side of a topographic barrier.

## Radiation budget

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

The [energy](energy.md) entering and leaving a system through [electromagnetic radiation](electromagnetic-radiation.md). A top-of-atmosphere [radiation budget](#radiation-budget) compares absorbed sunlight with outgoing [thermal radiation](electromagnetic-radiation.md#thermal-radiation). A latitude-dependent imbalance can be balanced by atmospheric and [ocean heat fluxes](#ocean-heat-flux) even when the global mean is zero.

## International Earth Rotation and Reference Systems Service

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

Publisher of [IERS Conventions (2010) by International Earth Rotation and Reference Systems Service](physics-bibliography.md#iers-conventions-2010-by-international-earth-rotation-and-reference-systems-service).

### Work by International Earth Rotation and Reference Systems Service

↑ **Parent:** [International Earth Rotation and Reference Systems Service](#international-earth-rotation-and-reference-systems-service)

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

[https://www.usgs.gov](https://www.usgs.gov)

### Work by United States Geological Survey

↑ **Parent:** [United States Geological Survey](#united-states-geological-survey)

## Geothermal gradient

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

The [geothermal gradient](#geothermal-gradient) is the rate at which [temperature](temperature.md) varies with depth. For steady one-dimensional [thermal conduction](heat.md#thermal-conduction) with constant [thermal conductivity](heat.md#thermal-conductivity) $k_T$, the magnitude of upward [heat flux](heat.md#heat-flux-density) is $q=k_T\,dT/dz$.

### Crustal mean temperature from conductive heat flow

↑ **Parent:** [Geothermal gradient](#geothermal-gradient)

For a crustal layer of thickness $h_c$ with constant [thermal conductivity](heat.md#thermal-conductivity) $k$, no internal heating and a linear conductive profile, the upward [heat flux](heat.md#heat-flux-density) magnitude $q$ obeys [Fourier's law](heat.md#fourier-s-law), $T(z)=T_s+qz/k$ with depth positive downward. Depth averaging gives

$$
\overline T_c=\frac1{h_c}\int_0^{h_c}T(z)\,dz=T_s+\frac{qh_c}{2k}.
$$

The basal excess temperature is $qh_c/k$, twice the mean excess. The formula requires a thickness assumption and a conductive interpretation of the measured flux. Hydrothermal advection, sedimentary thermal effects, internal production and nonlinear conductivity can invalidate a linear extrapolation.

## Planetary mantle

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

The [planetary mantle](#planetary-mantle) is the predominantly silicate rock region beneath a rocky planet's [crust](#planetary-crust). On geological timescales, solid rock can undergo [material creep](engineering.md#creep-deformation) and support [mantle convection](#mantle-convection) without wholesale melting.

### Secular cooling

↑ **Parent:** [Planetary mantle](#planetary-mantle)

A long-term decline in mean interior [temperature](temperature.md), distinct from a short transient, a local cold anomaly or a change in the pressure used for a [potential temperature](#potential-temperature). In a [mantle thermal budget](#mantle-thermal-budget), cooling requires heat loss to exceed internal generation plus heat entering from below. The rate depends on both the heat sources and the efficiency of transport.

### Mantle transition zone

↑ **Parent:** [Planetary mantle](#planetary-mantle)

The region of the terrestrial [mantle](#planetary-mantle) between major seismic discontinuities near 410 and 660 km depth, associated with mineral phase transformations.

### Mantle xenolith

↑ **Parent:** [Planetary mantle](#planetary-mantle)

A fragment of [mantle](#planetary-mantle) rock transported within magma to a shallower environment.

### Asthenosphere

↑ **Parent:** [Planetary mantle](#planetary-mantle)

The [asthenosphere](#asthenosphere) is a relatively weak region of the upper [mantle](#planetary-mantle) below the mechanically strong [lithosphere](#lithosphere). A seismic [low-velocity zone](#low-velocity-zone) often occurs in this region, particularly beneath oceanic [lithosphere](#lithosphere), but mechanical weakness and seismic [speed](classical-mechanics.md#speed) are distinct properties.

## Planetary crust

↑ **Parent:** [Geophysics](geophysics.md)

The [planetary crust](#planetary-crust) is the outer compositional rock layer of a differentiated rocky planet. Its thickness need not equal either the [lithosphere](#lithosphere)'s thermal thickness or its [effective elastic thickness](#effective-elastic-thickness).

### Crustal thickness

↑ **Parent:** [Planetary crust](#planetary-crust)

The distance from a body's surface to its [crust-mantle boundary](#mohorovicic-discontinuity), under a stated geometric convention. It differs from [lithosphere](#lithosphere) thickness and [effective elastic thickness](#effective-elastic-thickness). Converting a vertical column thickness to a layer thickness normal to a dipping interface requires its geometry.

### Mafic crust

↑ **Parent:** [Planetary crust](#planetary-crust)

[Crust](#planetary-crust) dominated by [mafic rocks](planetary-science.md#mafic-rock), commonly [basalt](planetary-science.md#basalt) and [gabbro](planetary-science.md#gabbro) or their metamorphosed equivalents.

<h3 id="mohorovicic-discontinuity">Mohorovičić discontinuity</h3>

↑ **Parent:** [Planetary crust](#planetary-crust)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohorovičić_discontinuity)

The seismic boundary between [crust](#planetary-crust) and [mantle](#planetary-mantle), commonly identified by a marked increase in elastic [wave speeds](wave-equation.md#wave-speed). It is a compositional boundary and need not coincide with the base of the [lithosphere](#lithosphere).

### Continental crust

↑ **Parent:** [Planetary crust](#planetary-crust)

The compositionally diverse, commonly thicker and more felsic [crust](#planetary-crust) of continental regions.

#### Crustal shortening

↑ **Parent:** [Continental crust](#continental-crust)

Horizontal contraction of [continental crust](#continental-crust), commonly accommodated by [folding](planetary-science.md#folding) and [thrust faults](#thrust-fault) and contributing to [tectonic thickening](#tectonic-thickening).

#### Craton

↑ **Parent:** [Continental crust](#continental-crust)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Craton)

A long-lived stable part of a continent, commonly with a thick, depleted [lithospheric mantle](#lithospheric-mantle) keel; that keel is distinct from the [continental crust](#continental-crust).

#### Tectonic thickening

↑ **Parent:** [Continental crust](#continental-crust)

Increase in [crust](#planetary-crust) or [lithosphere](#lithosphere) thickness through tectonic deformation, including [crustal shortening](#crustal-shortening) and stacking by [thrust faults](#thrust-fault).

#### Sedimentary cover

↑ **Parent:** [Continental crust](#continental-crust)

A near-surface layer of [sedimentary rocks](planetary-science.md#sedimentary-rock) over crystalline basement; its thickness can be zero and is not a fixed fraction of [continental crust](#continental-crust).

### Oceanic crust

↑ **Parent:** [Planetary crust](#planetary-crust)

The principally mafic compositional outer layer of an oceanic plate; it is only part of the thicker [oceanic lithosphere](#oceanic-lithosphere).

#### Sheeted dike complex

↑ **Parent:** [Oceanic crust](#oceanic-crust)

An assemblage of many intrusive [dikes](#dike), representing conduits feeding lavas at a [spreading ridge](#spreading-ridge).

### Basaltic crust

↑ **Parent:** [Planetary crust](#planetary-crust)

[Crust](#planetary-crust) dominated by [basalt](planetary-science.md#basalt) and compositionally related intrusive rocks.
